## Continuous and cross-cutting

Recurring duties that never finish, enforced by CI or `docs/release-runbook.md` (M0.14); per-release evidence lives in the release PR. Tick an X item once its automation or runbook has completed one verified cycle; if a cycle is missed by more than one period, untick it in the PR that notices and open an issue. `starts:` names the first milestone (dormant before it). Unmeasured numbers are PROVISIONAL.

### Quality and performance gates
- [ ] **X.01 PR accuracy smoke gate** _(cadence: every PR)_ - M1.50 `eval compare` on the 200-image synthetic smoke set blocks a merge on a mean quad IoU drop > 0.3 pt or a failure-rate rise > 0.5 pt vs `main` (PROVISIONAL until M1 measures noise); real-photo regressions are X.03.
- [ ] **X.02 PR instruction-count gate** _(cadence: PRs touching `imgproc`, `engine`, `codecs`)_ - gungraun (Linux only) compares head with `main` on hot kernels (downscale, warp, Sauvola, gain, LUT, decode glue): > 5% fails, 2% warns; a planted 10% slowdown must fail (M1.58, M4.56, M7.64).
- [ ] **X.03 Nightly wall-clock and accuracy suite** _(cadence: nightly on `main`)_ - criterion trends and the full accuracy suite (private dev tier via X.06) on a stable machine only (private-repo self-hosted runner or the Tier-M laptop)
- [ ] **X.04 Budget re-baseline** _(cadence: each milestone exit)_ - Re-run the harness on Tier-M and Apple silicon (M0.47); publish `docs/perf/baseline-<milestone>.md` (p50, p95 per stage at 12, 48, 100 MP, RSS) with the stage-sum check.
- [ ] **X.05 Device tracking** _(cadence: each release)_ - `docs/testing/devices.md` has one row per device, OS, GPU, webview and input with PASS, FAIL or UNMEASURED; release notes list every UNMEASURED cell, never a pass; shipping past one needs the owner's recorded delta (M13.42, A-9).

### Golden set, corpora and models
- [ ] **X.06 Golden-set stewardship** _(cadence: each release audit)_ - Private (B21, A-8). Staging: v0 >= 150 (>= 25 per slice, M1), v1 >= 500 (>= 50, before G2, M4), v2 >= 800 locked (>= 80, before 1.0, M13), plus a ~300 dev tier; n < 30 never published; a locked split used to tune is burned.
- [ ] **X.07 Golden-set refresh and audit** _(cadence: each exit; each detector change)_ - Compare strata with `docs/testing/golden-set.md`, label gaps and consented redacted failure reports (dev split only), second-annotate 20% of new labels.
- [ ] **X.08 Corpora and synthetic set upkeep** _(cadence: each milestone exit)_ - Regenerate the synthetic set with fixed seeds, verify `xtask fetch-corpus` hashes, re-check licences (SmartDoc and CORD CC BY 4.0, MIDV per subset, raw.pixls.us CC0 only; SROIE and DTD excluded)
- [ ] **X.09 Data-provenance log** _(cadence: every data PR; quarterly licence recheck)_ - Update the models-repo JSON-lines log (CSV and MD generated) in the same PR; status cleared, pending, blocked, banned or exception (owner-granted, A-7)
- [ ] **X.10 Model release discipline** _(cadence: every new model)_ - Model card, calibration refit (ECE <= 0.05 pooled, PROVISIONAL), int8 vs fp32 per platform (p95 corner-error rise <= 0.05% of diagonal, mean IoU delta <= 0.3 pt), ~12-25 MB bundle budget (B7), SHA-256 in `models.lock`

### Licence and supply chain
- [ ] **X.11 Licence policy** _(cadence: every PR; quarterly review)_ - `cargo deny` (bans AGPL, GPL, non-commercial, `heic`, `dssim-core`, `jpegxl-rs`, x264/x265), `reuse lint` 100%, DCO and `xtask check-native` stay green; each exception has a reason and date.
- [ ] **X.12 Dependency updates** _(cadence: weekly; toolchain quarterly)_ - Grouped Dependabot PRs (`cargo`, `github-actions`, from M3 `npm`) keep SHA pins (M0.08), reviewed within 14 days; after an `image`, `zune-*`, `tiff`, `ort` or `rusqlite` bump run X.01, X.02 and a fuzz smoke.
- [ ] **X.13 Release supply-chain hygiene** _(cadence: every release)_ - Every `uses:` SHA-pinned and `zizmor` clean, App or PAT expiry reminder (M0.13), `SHA256SUMS`, attestations and SBOM attached; draft, upload every asset, then publish (immutable).
- [ ] **X.14 Code-signing status** _(cadence: monthly until approved)_ - Track the SignPath application (M2.65, M2.66, M5.67-M5.68) and keep `docs/code-signing-policy.md` exact; once approved, verify Authenticode on every binary and installer per release.

### Native dependencies and watch lists
- [ ] **X.15 libheif and libde265 watch** _(cadence: daily via M0.10; starts: M6)_ - libheif patches only its latest release: never trail by more than one. Bump `native-deps.toml`, rebuild on all OSes, run HEIC and fuzz corpora, release a patch: critical/high 7 days (72 h target for critical)
- [ ] **X.16 Other native libraries** _(cadence: monthly; starts: M0)_ - libjpeg-turbo >= 3.1.4 with a CI version assertion (M0.45), dav1d, ONNX Runtime, libwebp, libjxl 0.12.x (from M11), and PDFium, LibRaw or libvips if B.01-B.08 land; same SLA as X.15; `docs/native-deps.md` tracks versions.
- [ ] **X.17 ort release-candidate watch** _(cadence: monthly; starts: M0)_ - `ort` is 2.0.0-rc.13: on stable, bump in its own PR with the int8 check and a golden regression; still an RC on 2027-06-30 (PROVISIONAL) reopens the backend ADR (rten, tract)
- [ ] **X.18 Pure-Rust HEIC re-evaluation** _(cadence: 2027-03-30, then 6-monthly)_ - M6.15 opens the issue. Candidates: heic-rs 0.1.1, heif-oxide; AGPL `heic` and `heic-decoder` stay banned.
- [ ] **X.19 GUI-stack watch list** _(cadence: monthly; starts: M0)_ - Tauri 3 stable and CEF (alpha, do not adopt early), wry leaving GTK3 webkit2gtk, Linux pinch issues (wry#544, tauri#13115), WebView2 and WebKitGTK versions; log checks in `docs/watchlist.md`
- [ ] **X.20 Distribution watch list** _(cadence: monthly; starts: M0)_ - Flathub AI policy (before B.20), Homebrew cask and Gatekeeper rules, GitHub runner changes (`macos-26-intel`, `windows-11-arm`, job caps), SignPath terms (X.14), Store fee (S.28), winget and Scoop criteria.

### Security and data safety
- [ ] **X.21 Fuzzing** _(cadence: nightly; 60 s PR smoke)_ - `cargo-fuzz` targets (M1.70, M2.61, M4.61, M6.63, M7.66, then every new parser and wrapper): 1 h per target nightly (PROVISIONAL), 60 s on PRs to codec/core crates; crashers replay as tests.
- [ ] **X.22 Decode limits and bombs** _(cadence: every release)_ - Own caps: pixels 100 MP (500 MP ceiling via Advanced or `--max-pixels`), bytes, frames, allocation, worker time 10 s + 1 s/MP; `heif_security_limits` stay on; bomb corpus per parser; `catch_unwind` per item (M0.31).
- [ ] **X.23 Sandbox regression** _(cadence: every release; starts: M6, M8, M9)_ - Re-run escape tests (open, socket, spawn, caps) on CI and hardware; `doctor` prints the achieved level (`appcontainer`, `job+token`, `landlock+seccomp`, `seccomp-only`, `sandbox_init`, `process-only`)
- [ ] **X.24 Data-safety invariants** _(cadence: PR subset, nightly matrix; starts: M2)_ - Never lose an original: PLAN 2.7 commit protocol, backup then restore byte-identical by SHA-256, fault injection (kill -9, ENOSPC, sharing violation, power loss), orphan-temp sweep.
- [ ] **X.25 Backup compatibility** _(cadence: every release; starts: M2)_ - Keep the backups, SQLite journal and sidecars of every released version as fixtures; the new build opens, migrates and restores them with equal SHA-256 and refuses a downgrade.
- [ ] **X.26 Vulnerability handling** _(cadence: continuous; yearly flow test)_ - `SECURITY.md` (M0.20): private advisories, acknowledge in 7 days and plan in 30 (PROVISIONAL), latest release supported; test the flow yearly with a test advisory.

### Privacy and legal
- [ ] **X.27 Privacy review** _(cadence: every release)_ - No telemetry or default network (network-blocked CI run, M5.48-M5.53, stays green); update check manual or opt-in weekly, install only on explicit click; Report issue sends only version, OS, backend and error code; logs path-redacted.
- [ ] **X.28 HEVC patent note** _(cadence: yearly; on codec change)_ - Re-read the published pool policies (Access Advance, Avanci, Sisvel, Via LA); keep the README and `SECURITY.md` patent paragraph and the `no-hevc` variant accurate; check patent statements before any new encoder.

### UI quality
- [ ] **X.29 Accessibility** _(cadence: UI PRs; each release manual)_ - Handles 48 px (never < 44), controls >= 24 px, single-pointer alternatives, keyboard access, no colour-only status, axe-core 0 serious or critical (M3.71)
- [ ] **X.30 i18n upkeep** _(cadence: every UI PR)_ - No hard-coded strings (lint), pseudo-locale and pseudo-RTL in CI (M5.47), logical CSS, plurals via the catalogue chosen in M3.50; with translations: string freeze 7 days before a tag, English fallback, 14-day PR review (PROVISIONAL)

### Docs, roadmap hygiene and process
- [ ] **X.31 Docs in the same PR** _(cadence: every PR)_ - A behaviour change updates user docs, CLI `--help` and README together; README tables come from harness output (aggregate only), PROVISIONAL on unmeasured numbers; screenshots refreshed each milestone.
- [ ] **X.32 ROADMAP.md hygiene** _(cadence: every PR; monthly stale review)_ - Tick only when implemented and verified, in the same PR. Never renumber or reuse an ID: retired IDs simply do not appear; later-obsolete items are struck through with `(dropped: reason)` and leave the totals.
- [ ] **X.33 PLAN.md upkeep** _(cadence: each ADR or new measurement)_ - Keep the decision-log rows (B1-B21), assumptions (C1-C7, A-1 to A-12) and the risk register accurate, match every ADR title against PLAN.md (M0.77), and ask the user before reopening any B-decision.
- [ ] **X.34 AI disclosure policy** _(cadence: every release)_ - One disclosure in README and CONTRIBUTING (M0.72): Claude Code assistance, human sign-off, no code knowingly copied from GPL, AGPL or non-commercial projects, derived algorithms reimplemented clean-room from papers.
- [ ] **X.35 Commits, release-plz and semver** _(cadence: every PR)_ - 0.x minors before 1.0; breaking changes to CLI flags, JSON, EditState, journal or backups go in `CHANGELOG.md` with migration text; strict semver for the CLI and on-disk formats from 1.0 (Rust API unstable until B.32)
- [ ] **X.36 Issue triage** _(cadence: weekly; label within 7 days)_ - Detection-failure reports ask for a redacted or synthetic sample (never a private image) and become synthetic regression tests where possible; consented samples join the golden dev split (X.07)
- [ ] **X.37 Platform CI parity** _(cadence: continuous)_ - Required PR checks on `windows-2025`, `macos-latest` (arm64) and `ubuntu-22.04` (glibc floor, never `ubuntu-latest`); arm64 and optional `macos-26-intel` jobs nightly (M0.04, M0.05); from M8 every exit gate passes on all three OSes.
- [ ] **X.38 Core stays UI-agnostic** _(cadence: every PR; quarterly review)_ - Tauri and wry only in `shell`; `core` needs only `serde` and `thiserror`, no tokio (M0.30, `xtask check-deps`).
- [ ] **X.39 Release runbook** _(cadence: every release)_ - Follow `docs/release-runbook.md` (M0.14): release-plz PR, tag with the App token, draft, upload every asset, attest, publish; then verify, install, upgrade and uninstall on a clean VM per OS, the 50-image dogfood set, and the X.24, X.27.
- [ ] **X.40 Retrospective** _(cadence: every release)_ - Size = part-time weeks (S 1-2, M 3-4, L 5-8, XL 9-16; M0, M1, M10, M11 = XL; PROVISIONAL); re-estimate, re-run X.04, update PLAN.md. Gates: G0 M1, G1 M2, G2 M4, G3 M7, G4 M10, G5 M12, G6 M8/M9, G7 M13, G8 M3, G9 M5, G10 M6, G11 M11.
- [ ] **X.41 Gate-miss protocol (A-4)** _(cadence: each missed exit gate)_ - Default: delay 1.0; Experimental or 1.x reopens B10 and is the owner's call, put with measured numbers, never a silent cut or relabel. Proposed cut order: dewarp, AVIF/JXL, multi-item.
- [ ] **X.42 Maintainer continuity** _(cadence: each release)_ - Keep `MAINTAINERS.md` (runbook link, who acts if the maintainer is unavailable) and a key inventory (GitHub App key, tokens, SignPath, updater key, Flatpak GPG key) with custody, rotation and recovery codes.

## Post-1.0 backlog (1.x)

Not scheduled or promised; nothing here blocks 1.0, and each item states its entry condition. To promote one, the retrospective (X.40) assigns it to a new milestone: append the new `M<n>.<nn>` to the B line (for example `-> M14.03`), keep the B ID, and tick it only when the milestone item is verified. All items honour B2, B12, B15 and B18 unless a user decision is named.

### Formats and I/O long tail
1.0 reads Tier 1 formats only; "all image formats" is unbounded, so the long tail is staged here by risk.
- [ ] **B.01 PDF page input** - Rasterise pages through sandboxed, hash-pinned PDFium (`pdfium-render`) or pure-Rust `hayro`; page picker, per-page EditState, export to multi-page PDF; worker pool only, render DPI cap 300 (PROVISIONAL). Entry: M6 pool and M11 PDF writer stable.
- [ ] **B.02 Animation and multi-page TIFF** - 1.0 writes only an opt-in first-frame or first-page copy with a notice (M6.34, D3). Add a page and frame picker, same crop for all frames, per-frame edits and re-encode to animated GIF, WebP, APNG and multi-page TIFF with caps.
- [ ] **B.03 Camera RAW embedded preview** - Read the largest embedded JPEG from RAW files and process it like a JPEG (a preview, not a develop). LGPL-2.1 crates (`rawler`, `rawloader`) fit B2 poorly, so an ADR picks an own TIFF-IFD extractor or a separate helper.
- [ ] **B.04 Full RAW develop out of process** - LibRaw 0.22.2 (LGPL-2.1 or CDDL-1.0) as a separately shipped shared library in a sandboxed worker: demosaic, white balance, colour matrix. Low priority (RAW develop does not help document scans); entry: B.03 shipped and user demand.
- [ ] **B.05 JPEG 2000 read** - `hayro-jpeg2000` (pure Rust) or OpenJPEG (C, BSD-2-Clause, worker only); check the licence against `deny.toml` first. Typical sources are scanned archives and JPX streams in PDFs (pairs with B.01).
- [ ] **B.06 SVG read** - Rasterise with `resvg` only: no external references (file or http), no scripts, capped output size; check the licence against `deny.toml` first (MPL-2.0 only for unmodified crates, M0.18).
- [ ] **B.07 PSD composite and `image-extras` formats** - PSD composite read (`psd` 0.3.5 is stale) and DDS, ICNS, PCX from `image-extras` (explicitly unfuzzed). Best effort: each format needs a fuzz target (X.21) and decode limits (X.22) before it is enabled.
- [ ] **B.08 Long-tail fallback engine** - libvips 8.18.x (LGPL-2.1+) or ImageMagick 7.1.x as an optional, unbundled fallback for files the built-in decoders cannot read, with a restrictive policy (no delegates, URL, MVG, MSL or PostScript); pixels return over shared memory from a sandbox.
- [ ] **B.09 HEIC lossless transforms and gain maps** - Lossless rotate and crop via ImageIO or WIC (WIC fails with `WINCODEC_ERR_UNSUPPORTEDOPERATION` if it must recompress); never x265. Gain maps: libheif ISO 21496-1 is unmerged (PR #1503); Ultra HDR JPEG needs an `ultrahdr-rs` licence check.
- [ ] **B.10 JBIG2 generic-region export, only if measured** - Compare lossless generic-region JBIG2 in PDF with TIFF CCITT G4 on the golden receipts before promising any saving; never lossy symbol-dictionary mode (it can substitute digits)
- [ ] **B.11 Opt-in output optimisers** - Moved from M11.06 and M11.20: measure mozjpeg and `oxipng` (levels 2-4) against libjpeg-turbo optimised Huffman and the `png` crate; adopt only if >= 5% smaller at equal SSIM (PROVISIONAL), licence permissive, link clean with `turbojpeg-sys`
- [ ] **B.40 OpenEXR read with tone-mapping** - `exr` (BSD-3-Clause) read to a tone-mapped SDR raster, output PNG or 16-bit TIFF, never replacing the source (D3); needs a fuzz target (X.21), decode limits (X.22) and a `deny.toml` check. Entry: user demand.
- [ ] **B.43 JPEG-to-JXL lossless recompression** - Fallback for the M11.28 stretch: `AddJPEGFrame` plus `StoreJPEGMetadata` when no pixel edit applies, after M11.15 patches the JPEG (no GPS restored); Advanced only. Entry: M11.28 misses its time-box.

### Detection, enhancement and quality
- [ ] **B.12 Lens-distortion correction with lens profiles** - Not in 1.0 (A-11). Match EXIF lens make and model to a profile and undo barrel or pincushion before geometry, with a manual slider otherwise.
- [ ] **B.13 Optional ML enhancement and shadow removal** - Fill the dormant ONNX slot with DocRes or DocShadow-SD7K (MIT code, ~120 MB) at ~768x1024 as a gain map; weights need a cleared provenance row (X.09); install from a local hash-pinned file only, no in-app download (B18)
- [ ] **B.14 GPU and NPU execution providers** - CoreML, DirectML or WinML and CUDA, opt-in after a per-model benchmark: enable only if >= 1.5x faster than CPU including transfer and cold load (PROVISIONAL) and inside the M0.34 int8 tolerance; CPU stays default and fallback.
- [ ] **B.15 Learning from the user's own corrections** - Step 1: recalibrate Strict, Balanced and Aggressive from local accept, adjust and skip history. Step 2, only if step 1 gains: on-device fine-tune of a small head. No telemetry or upload; one-click export and erase.
- [ ] **B.16 More classical enhancement operators** - Non-local-means denoise and Wolf or Su binarisation, added only if they beat the shipped Sauvola and NICK presets on H-DIBCO and on receipt Tesseract CER without lowering decimal-point accuracy.
- [ ] **B.42 Dewarp extras** - Editable mesh and book-spread splitting stay out of M12; add only if the G5 dewarp gate held and users ask; a mesh editor needs its own touch design and a single-pointer alternative (WCAG 2.5.7).

### Platforms and distribution
- [ ] **B.17 Windows on ARM64 first-class** - Best-effort `windows-11-arm` builds run from M0; promote with a native ARM64 NSIS installer and zip, ONNX Runtime win-arm64 verified (M0.34), NEON native libraries, milestone gates on the runner plus one real device; README claims only what was tested.
- [ ] **B.18 Linux ARM64 first-class** - The same promotion on `ubuntu-22.04-arm` (glibc floor kept): aarch64 AppImage, deb and rpm, Landlock and seccomp on the target kernels, and a WebKitGTK check on at least one real ARM64 device.
- [ ] **B.19 Intel Mac parity** - Choose per the M8 outcome (S.09): `rten`, a source-built ONNX Runtime, or `ort` `load-dynamic` with an own dylib, plus a universal (`lipo`) DMG; ONNX Runtime 1.30.0 ships no macOS x86_64 binary.
- [ ] **B.20 Flathub submission** - After 1.0 only (B17): hand-written manifest, human-opened PR, no AI-generated commit messages, descriptions or replies, honest AI disclosure; the policy flipped three times in 2026, so the own remote stays the fallback.
- [ ] **B.21 Apple notarisation and the main Homebrew cask** - Only if the user reopens B15 ($99 per year). Since 2026-09-01 the main cask tap rejects apps failing Gatekeeper, and self-submission needs 90 forks, 90 watchers or 225 stars on a repo older than 30 days.
- [ ] **B.22 winget manifest** - Extends M13.65: automate `komac` from the release workflow after the immutable release is published, for every release after 1.0.0 (M13.65 covers 1.0.0, tooling is M5.70); about 0.5 day (PROVISIONAL).
- [ ] **B.23 Scoop Extras** - Extends M13.65: submit to Scoop Extras (acceptance criteria unverified; about 0.5 day, PROVISIONAL); the own bucket `WelFedTed/scoop-auto-crop` is M5.70 and M13.65.
- [ ] **B.24 AUR source package** - Extends M9.23: an `auto-crop` source package only if a community maintainer volunteers; the checksum-pinned `-bin` package is M9.23.
- [ ] **B.25 Chocolatey, Snap and Nix** - Each only on demand. Snap strict confinement needs a portal-based file-access story that is not researched; a Nix package must respect the licence set and the `no-hevc` build option.
- [ ] **B.26 Distro packaging support** - Extends M6.73: beyond the documented `no-hevc` feature, support system libheif and libde265 instead of the bundled copies and the updater compiled out for distro packagers (Fedora, for example, keeps HEVC out of libheif).
- [ ] **B.27 Microsoft Store MSIX** - Extends M13.59-M13.61: per-release re-certification and listing updates after 1.0 (about 3 days of packaging, PROVISIONAL; review each release); the updater stays compiled out (B18). Dry run is M6.81, first submission M13.61; S.28 settles fees.
- [ ] **B.28 Tauri 3, WebKitGTK 6 and CEF evaluation** - Entry: Tauri 3 stable, wry on WebKitGTK 6 or GTK4, or a production-ready `tauri-runtime-cef`. Re-run the M0.56 workload on the Linux matrix, compare installer size (CEF embeds Chromium) and confirm plugin and updater parity.
- [ ] **B.29 Improved Linux touch and gestures** - An in-house pinch handler avoiding the WebKitGTK page-zoom hijack (wry#544, tauri#13115), possibly a `with_webview` zoom reset, `unsafe` confined to `shell`. Entry: M0.63 findings.
- [ ] **B.30 SLSA build L3 and reproducible builds** - Reusable-workflow layout for build L3 (M0.15 targets L2); test reproducibility by building twice on independent runners and comparing hashes, recording what is not deterministic (installer timestamps).
- [ ] **B.41 Signed apt and dnf repositories** - M9 ships deb and rpm notify-only, trusted by checksums and attestations; a signed repository on GitHub Pages or release assets gives upgrades through the package manager. Key custody joins the X.42 inventory; no paid hosting (B15).

### Data, interop and extensibility
- [ ] **B.31 Sidecar edit files and session export** - Opt-in JSON sidecar of the versioned EditState that follows a moved image (matched by content hash, no pixels), plus session-bundle export and import; off by default (clutters folders and synced directories).
- [ ] **B.32 Public library crate with a stable API** - Publish the library under the reserved crates.io name `auto-crop` (A-12; no `auto-crop-core`) with a semver-checked surface (EditState, History, detection traits), `cargo-semver-checks` in CI and docs.rs docs; dependencies stay permissive.
- [ ] **B.33 Plugin or scripting API** - Design first, build later. Cheapest: stable JSON in and out on the CLI. Heavier: WASM component plugins (for example wasmtime) with no filesystem or network by default; plugins are untrusted, capabilities explicit. Entry: B.32 stable.
- [ ] **B.34 Mobile app reusing the core crate** - Android or iOS shell over the UI-agnostic core; hard parts are ONNX Runtime and libheif per target and touch-first UI reuse. iOS needs the paid Apple programme (conflicts with B15: user decision)
- [ ] **B.35 Community translations and RTL locales** - Hosted platform with a free open-source tier (Weblate, Crowdin or similar; check terms) over the message catalogues, translator guide, one real RTL locale in CI (Arabic or Hebrew), font fallbacks.

### Models and datasets
- [ ] **B.36 Larger public receipt benchmark** - Existing sets under-represent long narrow receipts, thermal fade, partial frames and touching items: build a CC0 or CC BY benchmark with consent, redaction, per-file licences, scene-disjoint splits and a reproduction script.
- [ ] **B.37 Retrained detectors** - Retrain corner and orientation nets on the larger data (elongated-aspect pass, learned multi-item instances), refit calibration, pass X.09 and X.10. _.
- [ ] **B.38 Published accuracy leaderboard** - Publish aggregate metrics for every release (private golden aggregates plus the public benchmark) on the docs site, reproducible with `cargo xtask eval --public`, with error bars and per-slice numbers only where n >= 30.

### Security and assurance
- [ ] **B.39 OSS-Fuzz integration** - Apply with the cargo-fuzz targets of X.21 (PLAN 8.4.4). Entry: 1.0 shipped, targets stable for two releases, a maintainer able to triage reports; current acceptance criteria unverified. GitHub-hosted fuzzing (X.21) continues meanwhile.

## Explicit non-goals for 1.0

Plain bullets, deliberately not checkboxes; each was decided (B19 unless noted) and the architecture avoids painting the project into a corner for the ones that could return.

- **OCR and a searchable-PDF text layer (B19).** Large scope for a solo maintainer; the data model keeps page geometry and pixel provenance so a text layer can be added later; revisit only if the user reopens B19.
- **Scanner or camera capture: TWAIN, WIA, SANE, webcam (B19).** A per-OS driver and permission surface; users scan with their own software and open the file.
- **Watch-folder or background auto-processing (B19).** Unattended runs would bypass hold-until-reviewed (B4), the safety promise for a tool that overwrites originals.
- **A mobile app (B19, C3).** Different stores, signing costs and UI; the core stays UI-agnostic (X.38) so B.34 remains possible.
- **General photo editing: filters, retouching, colour grading (B19).** It dilutes the product; only crop, rotate, deskew, perspective, dewarp, document enhancement and conversion are in scope.
- **Radial lens-distortion correction in 1.0 (A-11).** Perspective correction and curved-page dewarp deliver the brief's distortion fixes; profile-based lens correction is the post-1.0 idea B.12.
- **Telemetry, analytics, accounts, cloud sync or any default network call (B18, C4).** Privacy-first; the only network use is an explicit manual or opt-in update check.
- **Silent or automatic updates (B18).** The tool overwrites user files, so updates are user-confirmed or notify-only.
- **Paid signing, certificates, hosting or services (B15).** The budget is $0: no Apple Developer fee, Azure Artifact Signing or EV certificate; only the user can reopen this.
- **AGPL, GPL or non-commercial dependencies and weights (B2).** Bans x265, x264, `jpegxl-rs`, the pure-Rust `heic` crate, `dssim-core`, DocTr, DocGeoNet and DocEnTr; GPL ideas are reimplemented clean-room from papers.
- **HEVC or HEIC encoding (B12).** Only decoding is bundled, which limits patent exposure and avoids GPL x265.
- **Preserving HDR gain maps, depth maps or Live Photo video in HEIC conversion (B12, C2).** They are dropped with a visible notice; lossless HEIC transforms are B.09.
- **ML enhancement or binarisation beyond the dormant model slot.** Flatten-then-threshold is classical, CPU-only and unvalidated on receipts, and candidate weights have unclear provenance; the slot stays empty until B.13.
- **Guaranteed Linux touch or gesture support (B8).** WebKitGTK behaviour is unmeasured and upstream issues are open, so it is best-effort (M9.36 records works, partial or fails and never blocks a release); mouse, trackpad and keyboard are guaranteed everywhere, touch on Windows and macOS.
- **First-class ARM64 on Windows or Linux, or Intel Macs, before 1.0 (C1).** Apple silicon (macOS arm64) is first-class; ARM64 on Windows and Linux and Intel Macs are best-effort until 1.0; promotion is B.17-B.19.
- **Tier 2 and Tier 3 input formats in 1.0.** PDF pages, per-frame animation, RAW, OpenEXR, JPEG 2000, SVG, PSD and the libvips fallback are B.01-B.08 and B.40; this reading of "all image formats" is an assumption the user can veto.
- **Shipping real user scans or the private golden set in the public repo (B21).** Only synthetic and permissively licensed public data are published.
- **Apple notarisation (B15) and a Flathub submission (B17) before 1.0.** Notarisation costs money, and Flathub's AI policy makes rejection a real risk; the own Flatpak remote comes first.
- **Mutating originals without a backup and a verified atomic replace (B3).** There is no unsafe write mode; retention of backups is configurable, but the backup itself is not optional.

## Spikes and open decisions

The tracking layer above the milestone items: each S item is a research follow-up from the decision log (section E) or an open question a plan depends on, and names its owner milestone. Tick one only when its result is recorded (an ADR under `docs/adr/`, a `docs/legal/` note or a policy file) with a go/no-go or decision line and the referenced milestone items are ticked; UNMEASURED counts as not proven. The maintainer never reopens a B-decision without asking the user.

### GUI stack and plumbing
- [ ] **S.01 Week-1 GUI spike: Tauri versus Slint** - Windows (Surface), macOS, Linux (Ubuntu 24.04, Fedora; Wayland, X11; touchscreen): folder drop, 24 MP pinch, pan, rotate, HiDPI, 500-image tiles, IPC; then the B8 rule once. Owner: M0 (M0.56-M0.70).
- [ ] **S.02 Slint contingency, only if triggered** - M3 becomes a UI rewrite: re-spike the winit 0.30 `DroppedFile` workaround (Wayland untested); missing RTL mirroring needs an owner waiver of B20, and the non-OSI Royalty-free 2.0 licence conflicts with B2. Owner: M0, then M3. Decider: user.
- [ ] **S.03 Re-run UNMEASURED GUI cells** - Every cell S.01 could not run is re-run on real hardware and appended to the ADR; a Linux touch failure in M9 is a documented B8 limitation (M9.36 records it, never blocks a release, no second Slint trigger). Owner: M8 (macOS), M9 (Linux).
- [ ] **S.04 Screen-reader testing on a webview canvas** - Check that the crop quad, handles, loupe and review grid expose roles, names and keyboard nudging through the webview. Owner: M3 (NVDA, Narrator), M8 (VoiceOver), M9 (Orca, best-effort, A-11).
- [ ] **S.05 Plumbing: 500-image tile serving and IPC** - Serve 256 px thumbnails and 512 px tiles through the custom URI scheme into a virtualised grid; measure scroll fps, RSS, payload choice and IPC p95 (tile, Channel). Owner: M0 (M0.65, M0.66, M0.71). _(gate: scroll >= 30 fps, PROVISIONAL)_
- [ ] **S.06 Plumbing: folder-drop edge cases** - Symlink loops, OneDrive placeholders (never hydrated), unreachable shares, 10k files, paths beyond 248 UTF-16 units, hostile Unicode names. Owner: M0 (M0.67, M0.68), adopted in M3, hardened in M5.

### Performance and inference
- [ ] **S.07 Benchmark and accuracy harness before GUI work** - criterion trends, gungraun gate, nightly wall-clock, real 12, 48, 100 MP files, Tier-M and Apple-silicon baselines, stage-sum check; re-measure every M0 number. Owner: M1.
- [ ] **S.08 Inference backend: ort, rten or tract; int8 per platform** - One backend behind a trait (op coverage, latency, cold load, size), then the int8 vs fp32 check on the real nets per OS. Owner: M0 (M0.32-M0.35), M4 (Windows run), M8 (macOS), M9 (Linux).
- [ ] **S.09 Intel Mac inference path** - Decide between `rten`, a source-built ONNX Runtime and `ort` `load-dynamic` with an own dylib, or stay best-effort, since ONNX Runtime ships no macOS x86_64 binary. Owner: M8; feeds B.19.

### Formats and HEIC
- [ ] **S.10 Real-device HEIC corpus** - iPhone (Display P3, HDR gain map, Live Photo, burst, 48 MP grid, portrait depth) and Android or Samsung (HEIC, 10-bit HDR), >= 6 per platform for the M0 plan, private with a hash manifest; M6.58 sets the gate counts. Owner: M0 (plan, M0.49), M6.
- [ ] **S.11 OS decoder benchmarks** - WIC and ImageIO against the own libheif build on the same files: decode time, colour fidelity, behaviour when the Windows HEVC extension is missing, and whether lossless HEIF rotate or crop is achievable. Owner: M6 (WIC), M8 (ImageIO).
- [ ] **S.12 HEIC colour handling check** - Read the ICC `prof` box first, then nclx or CICP; verify Display P3 to sRGB, PQ and HLG tone-mapping and the dropped gain-map, depth and Live Photo notices against real files. Owner: M6. _(gate: the M6 colour-difference gate, G10)_
- [ ] **S.13 Windows HEVC Video Extensions availability and price** - The claim that it is paid or OEM-only is unverified; check current Store status, because it decides whether the WIC fast path works on stock Windows and what the README says; never buy it (B15). Owner: M6.
- [ ] **S.14 HEIC batch memory budgeting** - A 48 MP HEIC is about 146 MB as RGB8 and libheif threads multiply with batch parallelism: design a byte-weighted admission budget that admits an oversize job alone, per-worker caps and a soak test. Owner: M6.
- [ ] **S.15 Per-OS decode sandbox** - Proof of concept on all three OSes, then the real implementation with a recorded level: AppContainer or job object plus restricted token (Windows), Landlock plus seccomp (Linux), `sandbox_init` (macOS). Owner: M0 (M0.21-M0.23), M6, M8, M9.

### Accuracy, enhancement and models
- [ ] **S.16 Receipt-specific stratified golden set** - Private (B21), built to the M0.50 and M0.51 policy: long narrow receipts > 4:1, thermal fade, partial frames, touching items, HEIC and other strata, with two-annotator noise-floor checks. Owner: M1 (v0), then X.06 and X.07.
- [ ] **S.17 Early 8:1 aspect test of the 256x256 heatmap net** - Measure the failure rate per aspect bucket (4:1, 6:1, 8:1, 10:1) before the M4 design freezes; synthetic strips overstate cleanliness, so treat the result as an input, not a verdict. Owner: M0 (M0.54, M0.55), consumed by M4.
- [ ] **S.18 Enhancement feasibility on real receipts** - Flatten vs Sauvola vs NICK with Tesseract 5 CER and H-DIBCO metrics on the golden receipts, sweeping the Sauvola window well beyond 31-51 px; flatten-then-threshold is unvalidated here. Owner: M1, gating M7.
- [ ] **S.19 Training pipeline design and compute plan** - Who trains, on what hardware, in which repo: a separate repo (`WelFedTed/auto-crop-models`), reproducible scripts, compute at $0 (own hardware or free notebook tiers whose terms permit the data). Owner: M1 (M1.75-M1.77), M4 (delivery).
- [ ] **S.20 Data-provenance audit of weights and datasets** - Policy and log (M0.24, M0.25); MakeACopy DocQuadNet-256 licence query (labelled Apache-2.0, GitHub says NOASSERTION; no reply in 14 days = dev-only)
- [ ] **S.21 Dewarp weight path decision** - B10 keeps dewarp in 1.0. Options: UVDoc-class weights (audit plus an owner-granted exception, A-7; ~30 MB, 0.4-1 s per page, PROVISIONAL; bundled or hash-pinned install-from-file) or own-data weights. Owner: M12, decided before M12 starts.

### Safe write and data safety
- [ ] **S.22 Windows retry on sharing violation** - Bounded retry with backoff (10-640 ms ladder, then retryable `FileInUse`) when antivirus, the indexer or a sync client holds the file during replace. Owner: M2. _(gate: fault test with a locked file passes and never loses the original)_
- [ ] **S.23 fsync of the parent directory after rename** - Unix: fsync the parent directory after rename; Windows: `FlushFileBuffers` on files and NTFS journalling for directories (PLAN 2.7 commit protocol); record it in an M2 ADR and test with fault injection. Owner: M2.
- [ ] **S.24 Backup links only on the same volume** - Reflink or hardlink only when source and store share a volume (safe because writes go through temp file plus rename); otherwise a re-hashed copy; decide Windows metadata preservation (`xattr` is Unix-only). Owner: M2.

### Legal, signing and distribution
- [ ] **S.25 HEVC patent read** - A short $0 read (pool policies or a free open-source legal clinic, never paid counsel) of whether shipping decode-only libde265 creates exposure and what `no-hevc` changes; drafts the README and `SECURITY.md` paragraph.
- [ ] **S.26 "Auto Crop" name check** - USPTO and EUIPO search in software classes 9 and 42, low priority; the name is also a Microsoft 365 command and a Mac App Store title, so it is always paired with the tagline. Owner: M0 (M0.28).
- [ ] **S.27 SignPath application outcome and timing** - Apply once v0.1.0 is public and the policy page is live; approval time is unpublished, so record the actual timing and any component that blocks eligibility. Owner: M2 (M2.65, M2.66), status M5 (M5.67-M5.68) and M13.02; tracked by X.14.
- [ ] **S.28 Microsoft Store fee status and MSIX feasibility** - Verify the fee (free for individuals since September 2025), runFullTrust and the worker process under MSIX, backups outside virtualised package data, certification timing.
